| PB2023 | FY2023 | Initiate airworthiness and ground and flight test approvals |
| PB2024 | FY2024 | Complete airworthiness and ground and flight test approvals |
| PB2025 | FY2024 | Complete airworthiness and ground and flight test approvals |
| PB2026 | FY2025 | Complete airworthiness and flight test approvals |
| PB2024 | FY2024 | Complete fabrication and subsystem integration |
| PB2025 | FY2024 | Complete fabrication and subsystem integration |
| PB2025 | FY2025 | Initiate and complete flight testing |
| PB2026 | FY2025 | Initiate and complete flight testing |

**The critical design review was promised for fiscal year 2022, then fiscal year 2023, then fiscal year 2024, then fiscal year 2024 again.** Completing fabrication and subsystem integration was a fiscal year 2024 plan in two consecutive books, and the fuselage was not finished until January 2026. **Initiating and completing flight testing was a fiscal year 2025 plan in two consecutive books.**

**The last of those is the one worth dwelling on.** The book published in June 2025 still lists initiating and completing flight testing of the demonstration aircraft among its fiscal year 2025 plans, four months after the zero-dollar change order that marks the pause and three months before the fiscal year ended \[[DARPA RDT&E justification, PB2026][ref_darpa_rdte_2026]\]. **Its fiscal year 2026 entry asks 4.000 million dollars to complete active-flow-control flight testing and data delivery**, against a first flight the same agency was describing as late 2027 five months later.

**That is not a contradiction and reading it as one would be unfair.** A justification book's plan text for the current fiscal year restates the plan on which that year's appropriation was enacted, which was settled long before the book was printed. **The plans column is a record of what was agreed, not a report of what is happening.** The right conclusion is narrower and more useful. **A budget justification is an excellent source for what a programme was authorised to do and a poor source for what it did**, and this article uses it only for the first.

## What the Data Would Change, and What It Has Already Changed

**An aircraft that has not flown has changed nothing by flying, and this article says so rather than crediting it with its intentions.** What CRANE has produced so far is a design, a set of tools and a contract structure, and the budget line states the intended product explicitly. Technologies, design tools and models developed under the programme are to be made available to all the services and to the civilian aerospace sector \[[DARPA RDT&E justification, PB2026][ref_darpa_rdte_2026]\]. **The deliverable is named as tools and models, not only as an aeroplane.**

**Two things have already changed and neither is aerodynamic.**

**The first is the contracting arrangement.** A DARPA technology demonstrator in which the contractor becomes a co-investor to complete the article is not the usual shape, and the agency's own account of why is that the new structure gets costs back to an affordable rate for the government, with the contractor taking over the platform for future test and development once the hard technical challenge is demonstrated \[[two-year flight delay][ref_defensenews_delay]\]. **The aircraft is to outlive the programme as a test asset**, which the programme manager said at the outset was a design goal and which the modular wing sets exist to serve \[[DARPA moves forward on the X-65][ref_darpa_news]\].

**The second is that the aircraft became the reason to hold the designation.** The X-65A designation was allocated in April 2023, between the Phase 2 award and the Phase 3 award, and the register carries it while the aircraft does not exist. **This series has now met several designations allocated to vehicles that never flew and one allocated to a vehicle that was never built.** The X-65A is a different case again. **It is allocated to a vehicle that is being built, slowly, with a first flight four and a half years after its designation.**

## Where the Framing Breaks Down

### The Century-Old Paradigm Is Younger Than It Sounds for One Axis

DARPA's release opens by observing that in December 1903 the Wright brothers flew the world's first fully controllable aircraft using wing warping, that virtually every aircraft since has used a system of movable external control surfaces, and that the X-65 breaks this century-old design paradigm \[[DARPA moves forward on the X-65][ref_darpa_news]\].

**The framing is half right and the half that is wrong is interesting rather than pedantic.** The 1903 aircraft was essentially a larger version of the 1902 glider, whose key control refinement was a movable vertical tail, and it carried a forward-mounted horizontal surface for climb and descent \[[1903 Wright Flyer][ref_nasm_flyer]\]. **So the Flyer used movable external surfaces for pitch and for yaw from the first flight.** For roll it did not. **It twisted the wing.**

**Wing warping is not a movable control surface. It is a morphing wing**, and the mechanism DARPA credits the Wrights with is the one axis where the original solution was not the paradigm being broken. Ailerons came later. **The paradigm is therefore 124 years old for two axes and rather younger for the third**, and the X-65's twin vertical tails carry conventional rudders among their fourteen surfaces, so the aircraft does not break the paradigm on the axis the framing invokes.

### The Amplification Curve Is Doing a Great Deal of Work

**This article's central arithmetic rests on a quantity it does not derive.** The amplification is treated as a parameter swept from ten to a hundred, drawn from the ranges the separation-control literature reports, and no attempt is made to predict it for this aircraft's geometry.

**That is honest and it is also the weakest link.** The amplification depends on the pressure gradient, the boundary-layer state, the Reynolds number, the effector geometry, its position relative to the separation line and the excitation frequency. **None of those is public for the X-65A and several are precisely what the aircraft exists to measure.** So the authority ratios in this article are conditional statements of the form that if the amplification is thirty then the deficit at Mach 0.7 is 43.3 percent, and they are not predictions.

### Reynolds Number Is Not the Problem, and the Equation Pass Said It Was

**The equation-density pass computed this aircraft's Reynolds number, compared it with the experiments whose thresholds this article had borrowed, found a gap of one to nearly three orders of magnitude, and concluded that the thresholds were the part most likely to be wrong at flight scale. The primary-reference pass found the report that settles it and the conclusion was too strong.**

The Reynolds number on a chord is the ratio of inertial to viscous forces. Let $\mathrm{Re}$ be that number, dimensionless, and $\mu$ the dynamic viscosity in pascal second, which for air follows Sutherland's law with constants $C_1$ in kilogram per metre second per root kelvin and $S_\mu$ in kelvin.

$$ \mathrm{Re} = \frac{\rho V \bar{c}}{\mu}, \qquad \mu\left( T \right) = \frac{C_1 \, T^{3/2}}{T + S_\mu} $$

with $C_1$ of $1.458 \times 10^{-6}$ and $S_\mu$ of 110.4 kelvin. **That returns 1.7894 times ten to the minus five pascal second at sea level**, against the published 1.789 times ten to the minus five. **At Mach 0.7 and thirty thousand feet on a chord of 1.750 metre the X-65A flies at 11.44 million.**

**Active separation control by oscillatory excitation was demonstrated above that Reynolds number in the year 2000, and this article did not know it.** Seifert and Pack tested the method at chord Reynolds numbers as high as forty million and state in their summary that it was proved successful in delaying boundary layer separation and reattaching separated flows at chord Reynolds numbers of order ten million \[[Seifert and Pack 2000][research_seifert_pack_2000]\]. **Their baseline flow was made fully turbulent deliberately, to remove transition as a variable, and they report that the Reynolds number has a very weak effect on the model pressure distributions and spectra regardless of Mach number or sweep.**

| Study | Reynolds number | The X-65A in flight over it |
|---|---|---|
| Munday and Taira 2018 | 2.30 times ten to the 4 | 497.6 |
| Feero et al. 2015 | 1.00 times ten to the 5 | 114.4 |
| Seifert et al. 1996, low | 1.50 times ten to the 5 | 76.3 |
| Jones and Englar 2003 | 5.00 times ten to the 5 | 22.9 |
| Seifert et al. 1996, high | 6.00 times ten to the 5 | 19.1 |

**The X-65A flies at 0.715 times that experiment's sixteen million and 0.286 times the forty million it quotes.** It is inside the demonstrated range, not beyond it. **So the honest statement is the narrow one.** The specific threshold values the equation pass borrowed do come from experiments at a hundred thousand and twenty-three thousand, and transferring a threshold is a different matter from transferring a phenomenon. **But the phenomenon is not in doubt at this aircraft's Reynolds number and the article should not have implied it was.**

### What the Flight-Reynolds Primary Actually Reports, Which Is Better News

**The same report quotes the oscillatory momentum coefficients it used, and its nomenclature defines the coefficient as the jet momentum over chord times dynamic pressure, which is this article's definition exactly.**

| Case the report quotes | Oscillatory momentum coefficient | This article's figure over it | Concentration still needed |
|---|---|---|---|
| lowest quoted, Mach 0.25 | 0.03 percent | 3.150 | none |
| slot at 64 percent chord, Reynolds 16 million | 0.09 percent | 1.050 | none |
| reduced frequency 1.0 | 0.12 percent | 0.788 | 1.27 |
| wall pressures, Reynolds 16 million, Mach 0.25 | 0.13 percent | 0.727 | 1.38 |
| reduced frequency 0.5 | 0.32 percent | 0.295 | 3.39 |

**The wing-referenced coefficient this article computes, 0.0945 percent, sits inside the range that report used, 0.03 to 0.32 percent.** It reaches 2 of the 5 quoted cases with no concentration at all, and the largest needs a factor of 3.39. **The report states its own uncertainty on that coefficient as plus or minus 25 percent of the quoted values**, which is a primary-sourced error bar and not an assumption of this article.

**And at that report's own Mach number of 0.25 the bleed budget delivers 0.5579 percent, which is 1.74 times its highest quoted value.** So the air budget is not marginal at the condition where the method was demonstrated. **It is marginal only where this aircraft wants to fly**, and the factor between the two is 5.903, which is the Mach dependence derived above and nothing else.

### And the Primary Names This Article's Keystone as the Open Problem

**The report's own proposals for future work include overcoming the lack of sufficient control authority, especially at high speeds** \[[Seifert and Pack 2000][research_seifert_pack_2000]\].

**That is the finding this article derived, named as unsolved twenty-five years before the X-65A was due to fly.** The authority ratio falls as the square of the Mach number because the engine delivers a roughly fixed momentum while the aerodynamic forces to be overcome grow with the square of the speed. **A primary that tested the method at this aircraft's Reynolds number, at a third of its Mach number, and concluded that high-speed control authority was the thing still to be found, is the strongest available independent support for the conclusion.**

**It is also a caution about novelty.** This article derived the Mach dependence and its exact minimum, and did not derive the observation that high-speed authority is the binding constraint. **That observation is in the literature and this article found it only in the pass that went looking for primaries.**

### The Bleed Penalty Is Asserted Rather Than Derived

**Taking air from a compressor costs thrust and this article does not compute how much.** The mass-flow accounting is exact, being 8 percent of a core flow of 10.0 kilogram per second, which is a small fraction of total engine flow at a bypass ratio of 3.3. **The thrust consequence is not exact**, because it depends on the turbine work no longer available to drive the fan, on the cycle's rematching, and on the control schedule the engine's digital controller applies.

**A defensible lower bound is that the thrust loss is at least the lost mass flow's share, and the true figure is larger.** This article declines to put a number on it. **The single-stage centrifugal architecture makes the answer worse than a multi-stage axial's would be**, because the bled air has had the whole compression done to it, and that much follows from the architecture the NASA report describes \[[FJ44 turbofan engine test][ref_fj44_test]\].

### Suction Is in the Official Definition and Not in This Analysis

**The budget justification defines active flow control as control mechanisms which alter the aerodynamic flow field through ejection or suction of fluid via an orifice on a lifting body** \[[DARPA RDT&E justification, PB2026][ref_darpa_rdte_2026]\]. **The phrase includes suction and this article's whole momentum accounting assumes blowing.**

A suction device removes low-momentum air rather than adding high-momentum air, and its figure of merit is a flow coefficient rather than a momentum coefficient. **The identity that makes the amplification exact does not hold for it**, because there is no jet momentum in the denominator. **Nothing published says which the X-65A uses**, and a system that uses both would need both accountings. This article's analysis applies to the blowing case and says so.

### One Vehicle Still Cannot Do Everything

**The differencing argument above is strong and it has a limit.** Locking a conventional surface and commanding an effector instead changes the aircraft's aerodynamics in ways beyond the intended substitution. **A locked surface is still a surface, sitting in a position, with its gaps and its hinge line**, and it is not absent. An aircraft designed without conventional surfaces would have a different shape, lower weight and no gaps.

**So the X-65A measures the authority of flow control on an airframe built to accommodate both systems**, which is not the same as the authority of flow control on an airframe built for flow control alone. **The programme's own framing acknowledges this by calling the conventional surfaces training wheels**, and the honest reading is that the aircraft measures a lower bound on what a purpose-built vehicle would achieve and an upper bound on the weight saving.

## The Contemporary Literature

The survey behind this article holds **3,709 records** after gating and de-duplication, drawn from two sweeps that asked 176 questions of the reports server, 25 of the defence registry and 35 of the bibliographic index. **1,464 of them, 39.5 percent, are report primaries**, meaning items served by the National Aeronautics and Space Administration's technical reports server or registered under the Defense Technical Information Center's prefix, and the remaining 2,245 are journal and conference records. 3,572 records carry a usable year, running from 1927 to 2027 with a median of 2007. **1,274 of those, 35.7 percent, predate the year 2000, and 1,096, or 30.7 percent, are 2015 or later.**

**The heaviest decade is the 2010s, with 946 records, 26.5 percent of the dated set**, and the decade distribution has a second mass in the 1970s and 1980s which is the boundary-layer-control and circulation-control era this subject keeps returning to.

**The primary-reference pass moved both the count and the share and it is required to report both.** Report primaries went from 1,387 to 1,464, a share of 38.1 to 39.5 percent, and the period count of records predating the year 2000 from 1,205 to 1,274, a share of 34.4 to 35.7 percent. **The median year did not move**, staying at 2007, and the earliest record moved back ten years to 1927. **Reference definitions went from 3,658 to 3,804, of which ten were written by hand for primaries this pass went looking for and four of those ten were read in full.**

| Decade | Records | Share of the dated set |
|---|---|---|
| 1920s | 2 | 0.1 percent |
| 1930s | 3 | 0.1 percent |
| 1940s | 29 | 0.8 percent |
| 1950s | 69 | 1.9 percent |
| 1960s | 80 | 2.2 percent |
| 1970s | 376 | 10.5 percent |
| 1980s | 347 | 9.7 percent |
| 1990s | 368 | 10.3 percent |
| 2000s | 774 | 21.7 percent |
| 2010s | 946 | 26.5 percent |
| 2020s | 578 | 16.2 percent |

**Both the count and the share are stated because they move in opposite directions.** A pass that adds older primaries raises the period count while lowering the recent share, and reporting only the share would read as a regression when it is the directive working.

**Every record the gate admitted is cited below.** The clusters are ordered with the specific before the general ones they are special cases of, because a record matching several gates is displayed under the first, and a broad cluster placed early takes the records a narrow one was written for.

### This programme, this aircraft and this contractor

**No records.** The sweep retrieved 18,432 records and not one of them names this programme, this aircraft or this contractor. **That is a measurement about the subject rather than about the sweep**, and the probe that predicted it is in the source base below.

### The medium, admitted by the shared atmosphere family alone

**1 records.** \[[Air Force Test Pilot School Edwards Afb Ca 1962][research_airforcetestpilotschooledwardsafbca_1962]\]

## The Source Base

### The Pool, and What the Gate Did to It

Two sweeps retrieved **20,430 distinct records**. The homonym store refused 1,535 of them against incidents recorded by earlier articles in this series, leaving 18,895. **The subject gate admitted 3,981 and refused 14,914, which is 21.1 percent kept.**

**One store tag was switched off for this article and the reason is on the record.** The `smart-actuators` family exists because an earlier article's bare pattern for the word dielectric deleted a paper on active flow control of a wing section in stall flutter by dielectric barrier discharge plasma actuator. **That is exactly this article's subject**, so the family is allowed through, and saying so here is required because a filter switched off silently is indistinguishable from a filter that never existed.

### What the Reports Server Was Asked, and What It Holds

The reports server was asked 176 questions across two sweeps. **It reported 22,841 matches and returned 17,518, which is 76.7 percent.** 8 questions in the first sweep hit the retrieval depth and were walked deeper in the second, where 6 still did not reach the bottom.

**Four questions the server holds nothing at all for are worth naming**, because a null answer from a registry is a measurement. They are `hingeless aerodynamic control effector`, `technology demonstrator aircraft programme`, `Coanda effect jet attachment` and `mixing enhancement jet actuator`. **The first of those is the finding and it is treated separately below.**

### The Homonyms, Measured Before the Sweep Was Written

**This article's vocabulary is the worst the series has met and the measurement is the reason the sweep looks as it does.** Ten results were read from the bibliographic index for each probe and counted by hand.

| Probe | Results read | On subject | What the rest were |
|---|---|---|---|
| `CRANE` | 6 | 0 | a logging-crane fatality, a mobile-gantry-crane fatality, a civil-engineering journal, `CRANE-tools.org` and two collections of nursery rhymes |
| `novel effectors` | 10 | 1 | fungal, oomycete and oncological effectors, the one exception being a 2026 paper on hypersonics and directed energy |
| `effectors` | 9 | 1 | TAL effectors, rust effectors, smut fungi and the effectors of plant-defence suppression |
| `end effector` | 10 | 0 | industrial robot wrists and upper-limb kinematics |
| `actuator` | 10 | 0 | road-vehicle air-brake actuator test procedures, eight of the ten |
| `momentum coefficient` | 10 | 1 | tangential momentum accommodation in microchannels, angular momentum coupling, momentum exchange in rarefied flow, and an evolutionary optimiser with a momentum and adaptive decay coefficient, twice |
| `blowing coefficient` | 10 | 0 | elliptic equations with a blowing-up coefficient, plus `Blue-blowing` and `BLOWING ROCK LANDMARKS` |
| `control authority` | 10 | 0 | bibliographic authority control, at the National Archives and in Italy |
| `control effectiveness` | 10 | 0 | internal-control auditing and diamondback-moth pest management |
| `separation control` | 10 | 1 | chemical separation processes, dust and pollution control |
| `fluidic oscillator` | 10 | 2 | an antisolvent crystalliser and the American Water Works Association standard for cold-water meters, in two editions |
| `supercirculation` | 8 | 3 | the climate supercirculation of the Earth |
| `Aurora Flight Sciences` | 10 | 2 | Mercury-Atlas 7, a damselfly, a Brazilian philosophy journal and a casket company |
| `X-65` | 10 | 0 | front matter and acknowledgements, which is what the substance test exists to refuse |
| `active flow control` | 10 | 10 | nothing off subject |
| `sweeping jet actuator` | 10 | 10 | nothing off subject |
| `hingeless control` | 10 | 10 | nothing off subject in the bibliographic index, **and 72 rotorcraft records on the reports server** |

**Three of those rows governed research decisions.** The programme is never searched for by its acronym. The word `effector` is never used unqualified. **And the article's own keystone parameter was searched for and found almost absent**, which is the next subsection.

### The Keystone Parameter Is Almost Never in a Title

**Twelve titles in a pool of nearly twenty thousand name the momentum coefficient or the blowing coefficient at all, and the gate admits eleven of them.** The display cluster for the parameter holds 17 records because it also matches patterns for the blowing rate and for actuator effectiveness.

**That is not a failure of the query and it is not a thin literature.** Every paper in active flow control uses the momentum coefficient, and the review this article leans on devotes a numbered section to it \[[Taleghani and Hosseini 2024][research_review_afc_2024]\]. **The parameter is a method rather than a subject, so it appears in equations and in axis labels and not in titles.** An earlier article in this series distinguished a query that failed from a literature that was thin. **This is a third case, in which both the query and the literature are healthy and the quantity is invisible to a title search.**

### A Homonym That Depends on Which Registry You Ask

**`hingeless control` returned ten results from the bibliographic index of which ten were about flow-control effectors, and seventy-two from the reports server of which every one was a helicopter.**

A hingeless rotor is a blade retention without flap and lag hinges, which is a structural subject with a large literature from the 1960s and 1970s. A hingeless control effector is an aerodynamic surface replaced by a jet. **The same two words name two unrelated subjects and which one you get depends on which registry you ask.**

**This series has recorded that two registries hold different literatures. This is the first time it has recorded that they hold different meanings for one phrase.** The gate therefore carries a rotorcraft exclusion that its first version did not, and the exclusion was added because a probe was read rather than because an audit failed. **The right anchor differs by registry**, being `hingeless control` for the journals and `control effectors` for the reports, and the second returns the aerodynamic control effectors, the passive porosity effectors and the active flow effectors that the first sweep had no word for.

### What the Audit Found, Because It Was Read in Both Directions

**The gate was run three times and the two-sided audit changed it twice.**

**The first audit's refused sample contained a co-flow jet airfoil paper and a pneumatic spoiler report**, so an entire effector family and a whole class of pneumatic device had no anchor at all. Both were added, with microjets, microvanes and microtabs, which the same reading turned up.

**The second audit's refused sample contained a reconfigurable control design for the X-33**, and following it found fifty-nine records on control allocation of which forty were being refused. **Twenty-eight effectors on three axes is an allocation problem by construction**, so that cluster exists because a random sample of refusals was read and not because the sweep was planned well.

**Neither finding would have appeared in any summary statistic.** A narrow gate reports a small corpus, which reads as a thin literature rather than as a defect.

### A Guard Defeated by a Hyphen, Which the Shared Library Cannot Repair

**The gate's rotorcraft exclusion was written with a space and the reports server writes the phrase with a hyphen, so the guard did not fire and admitted the record it existed to refuse.**

The shared library flattens intraword hyphens so that an anchor written `wind tunnel` still matches `wind-tunnel`. **It does so only after the unflattened pattern has failed.** A guard is a negative lookahead, so a guard that fails to fire is a guard that admits, and the record is admitted on the first attempt before flattening is ever tried. **The library's repair is correct for match patterns and structurally cannot help exclusions.**

**Two further guards leaked when the same refusal cases were re-tested with their spaces hyphenated**, and they had passed the regression test because the test used the spelling the probe happened to return. Every guard phrase now goes through a helper that makes its internal spaces tolerate a separator, and the hyphenated variants are permanent regression cases. **A test that only uses one spelling is a test of the probe and not of the guard.**

### What the Primary-Reference Pass Found, and What It Had to Withdraw

**Seven places in this article cited one review and every threshold in it was second-hand.** The regime boundary of three to five percent, the reduced-frequency band, the measured response at 0.08 percent, the reattachment at 0.34 percent, the modified-coefficient thresholds, the velocity-ratio condition, the caution against comparing momentum coefficients across configurations and the attribution of the definition to Poisson-Quinton were all read through the review rather than from the work that established them. **A pass that exists to prefer primaries has to start by listing what is not one.**

**Nine of those originals were located and four could be read in full.** The four are agency reports. **Every journal item the review names is behind a paywall**, so the upgrade from second-hand to first-hand was available only where the work was also published as a report. **That asymmetry is the practical reason this corpus prefers the report literature**, and it is stated rather than worked around.

**The most valuable of the four changed a conclusion.** A report on separation control at flight Reynolds numbers, read in full, demonstrated the method at chord Reynolds numbers as high as forty million and quotes the oscillatory momentum coefficients it used \[[Seifert and Pack 2000][research_seifert_pack_2000]\]. **The equation-density pass had argued that this article's threshold values came from experiments one to nearly three orders of magnitude below flight scale and were therefore the part most likely to be wrong.** The report shows the phenomenon demonstrated above this aircraft's Reynolds number, not below it, **so the strong form of that argument is withdrawn in the body rather than quietly softened.**

**And it names this article's own keystone as an open problem**, proposing among its future directions that the lack of sufficient control authority at high speeds be overcome. **The conclusion now says the constraint was derived here and discovered elsewhere.**

### What the Targeted Sweep Bought, Which Was Less Than It Asked For

**The pass asked 86 new questions of the two report registries and the return was modest.** Research records rose from 3,636 to 3,709, report primaries from 1,387 to 1,464, and the primary share from 38.1 to 39.5 percent.

**Both the count and the share rose, which the genre document warns is unusual.** A reference pass normally raises the period count while lowering the recent share, because it adds older primaries faster than recent ones and the denominator grows. **Here the sweep was aimed at one registry's literature rather than at the subject broadly**, so primaries arrived faster than anything else and both numbers moved the same way. The period count rose from 1,205 to 1,274 and the period share from 34.4 to 35.7 percent.

| Cluster | Records before | Report primaries before | Records after | Report primaries after | Change in share |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Where the air comes from, and what taking it costs | 77 | 9.1 percent | 80 | 12.5 percent | +3.4 points |
| The devices themselves | 900 | 13.6 percent | 906 | 14.2 percent | +0.6 points |
| Joined, tandem, diamond and tailless | 404 | 21.3 percent | 406 | 21.7 percent | +0.4 points |
| Separation and its control | 388 | 22.4 percent | 393 | 23.7 percent | +1.3 points |
| The mechanical control system this aircraft would delete | 86 | 25.6 percent | 97 | 35.1 percent | +9.5 points |
| The momentum coefficient itself | 15 | 26.7 percent | 17 | 35.3 percent | +8.6 points |
| Reynolds number and scale | 180 | 34.4 percent | 181 | 34.8 percent | +0.4 points |
| The fluid mechanics underneath | 405 | 41.0 percent | 421 | 43.2 percent | +2.2 points |
| Active flow control as a subject | 474 | 43.0 percent | 478 | 43.5 percent | +0.5 points |
| Control without a hinge | 88 | 50.0 percent | 88 | 50.0 percent | +0.0 points |
| Blowing, the Coanda surface and the jet flap | 837 | 54.5 percent | 859 | 55.8 percent | +1.3 points |
| Allocating a demand across effectors | 53 | 54.7 percent | 54 | 55.6 percent | +0.9 points |
| Identifying an aircraft from its flight data | 221 | 58.8 percent | 223 | 59.2 percent | +0.4 points |
| Controlling a vortex | 41 | 70.7 percent | 42 | 71.4 percent | +0.7 points |
| Demonstrators and research aircraft | 170 | 78.2 percent | 184 | 79.9 percent | +1.7 points |

**The cluster the pass most wanted to fix moved least, and that is the finding.** The air budget stood at 9.1 percent of 77 records and now stands at 12.5 percent of 80. **Thirty-five dedicated questions across both registries bought three records and three primaries.**

**The reason is measurable and it is not the sweep's.** Asking the reports server for a secondary air system returns two hundred and seven records and they are the proceedings of a seal workshop, because inside a turbine engine secondary air means internal cooling. Asking it for an aircraft air supply system returns electric-propulsion impedance measurements and direct-current power supplies. Asking the defence registry what bleed air costs in engine performance returns two hundred records of which three pass the subject gate, and none of those three is about bleed air. **What a bleed costs an engine is known to the companies that build engines and is not in either public registry.**

**So the article's refusal to put a number on the thrust penalty is not a gap in its research.** It is a gap in the record, and the pass that went looking confirmed it rather than closing it.

**Two clusters did move substantially.** Conventional actuation went from 25.6 to 35.1 percent, a gain of +9.5 points, because hinge moments and actuation power are exactly what the agency report literature of the 1960s and 1970s is full of. **The effectors moved from 13.6 to 14.2 percent and control without a hinge did not move at all**, staying at 50.0 percent, which says the device literature and the hingeless-control literature are genuinely journal-held and no amount of asking a report server will change that.

### The Programme's Own Ancestor Has No Report Literature

**The CRANE manager named Micro Adaptive Flow Control as the work CRANE descends from, and neither registry holds a report about it.** The reports server returns, for that phrase, two summer faculty fellowship programmes from 1995, a Spacelab life-sciences experiment and a paper on the importance of properties in modelling. The defence registry returns one hundred and twenty-one records of which two pass the gate and both are about adaptive *structures* rather than adaptive flow control.

**The only trace of the programme this article could find is a single conference paper, behind a paywall, whose title is its lessons learned** \[[MAFC lessons learned 2004][research_mafc_lessons_2004]\]. **A DARPA programme leaves no report literature in a NASA registry because it was never a NASA programme**, and the defence registry holds what its contractors delivered rather than what the agency learned. **The ancestor of an aircraft is harder to find than the aircraft.**

### Serendipity, Which Is Worth Recording Because It Is Not Method

**Three of the better records in the allocation cluster came from a question about something else.** Asking the defence registry about secondary power extraction returned `Tailless Aircraft Control Allocation` of 1997 and two reports on robust nonlinear control of tailless aircraft from 1999, which are the nearest prior art to a twenty-eight-effector allocation problem and have nothing to do with secondary power. **The query matched on the word aircraft and the gate kept them on their subject.**

**That is luck and it is reported as luck.** A sweep that found its best records for one cluster while asking about another has not demonstrated a method, and the honest inference is that the allocation cluster should have been asked directly.

### The Same Work Twice, Once With a File and Once Without

**The reports server holds that report under two identifiers and only one of them carries the document.** A search returns both. The first has a record and no attachment, so a request for its full text returns nothing at all, and the second carries the portable document and a plain-text rendering of it. **A null answer from a registry is usually a measurement about the literature. This one was a measurement about the catalogue**, and the only way to tell the difference was to ask the detail endpoint which identifier had a file.

### The Sweep Was Targeted by Measurement

**The per-cluster share of report primaries was computed before the sweep was written, and five clusters came back thin.** The air budget stood at 9.1 percent of its records, the effectors at 13.6 percent, the planform at 21.3, separation at 22.4 and conventional actuation at 25.6. **The air budget was the worst and it underwrites a claim the article makes**, being the lower bound on the thrust a bleed costs, so it was asked first.

**And the questions were written in the report literature's own vocabulary rather than the journals'.** A reports server asked for a fluidic oscillator is being asked the modern journal name for an old device. **Boundary layer control, blowing, suction, bleed, the augmentor wing, the externally blown flap and the jet flap are what the 1960s and 1970s wrote down**, and this article's median year before the pass was 2007, which says the first sweep leaned modern.

### What This Article Read in Full

**Read in full.** The register's addendum, the encyclopedia entry, the DARPA programme page, the DARPA news release of 3 January 2024, the contractor's aircraft page, the Defense News report of 20 November 2025, the Smithsonian collection record for the 1903 Flyer, the NASA technical memorandum on the FJ44-3A, the open-access review of flow separation control, the CRANE entries in all seven DARPA justification books from the request for fiscal year 2020 to the request for fiscal year 2026, **and four agency reports on separation control retrieved in the primary-reference pass**, being the flight-Reynolds-number lessons-learned report, the flap-separation report, the slat-shoulder report and the amplitude-scaling workshop paper.

**Read to the abstract only, and quoted for nothing inside it.** Reddy and Woszidlo 2024, which is closed access, held by no repository, and whose two scaling parameters both registries render as a placeholder \[[Reddy and Woszidlo 2024][research_reddy_woszidlo_2024]\].

**Held at registry-record strength, named because the review names them and quoted for nothing.** Seifert and others 1996 in the Journal of Aircraft, Munday and Taira 2018 in the AIAA Journal, Feero and others 2017 in Experiments in Fluids, the MAFC lessons-learned paper of 2004, the flow-control terminology chapter of the 2009 AIAA volume, and Wygnanski's 1997 conference paper. **Six paywalled items whose existence, venue and year are confirmed and whose contents are not.** Where this article states a number those works established, it states it on the review's authority and says so.

**One citation the review gives could not be matched.** It attributes a reattachment threshold to Feero and others in 2015 and the registry holds that work in 2017. **The year is reported as the registry gives it and the discrepancy is left standing rather than resolved**, because resolving it would need the review's own bibliography, which the open-access text renders as author-year strings without identifiers.

**Retrieved as records and not read.** The remaining 3,709 research records, which are cited as a body of literature in the clusters above and not quoted.

## Epistemic State

**Historical fact, from documents read in full.** The X-65A designation was allocated on 24 April 2023 to Aurora Flight Sciences, with the engines cell reading `1 Williams FJ44-3A` and DARPA as sponsor. CRANE began with three Phase 0 contracts in mid-2020 to Aurora Flight Sciences, Lockheed Martin and Georgia Tech Research Corporation, all three reporting twenty-five offers received. A Phase 2 and Phase 3 contract was placed on Aurora on 7 December 2022 and carried 94,372,238.00 dollars in eighteen actions ending 16 January 2025. DARPA paused the programme early in 2025, restructured it so the contractor became a co-investor, finalised that in August 2025, and expects first flight in late 2027. The aircraft has fourteen flow-control effectors, fourteen conventional control surfaces, four interchangeable wing sets, a thirty foot span and a limit of Mach 0.7. The programme manager named Micro Adaptive Flow Control as its ancestor and said he had managed that programme from 1999 to 2003.

**Engineering analysis, this article's own, checkable from the equations given.** The amplification identity. The exact cancellation of altitude from the bleed-fed momentum coefficient. The identical nozzle pressure ratio across the envelope. The Mach-number factor, its exact minimum at Mach 1.4142, and the closed form of that minimum. The authority ratio, the crossover Mach number and the amplification required to match a conventional surface. The concentration factor and the effector sizing. The slot areas. The reduced-frequency band mapped onto this aircraft's chord. The linear and second-order term counts and the flights they imply. **All of it is conditional on parameter values that are not published, and every such parameter is swept.**

**Very likely a rediscovery.** The altitude result. A 2024 paper asks whether momentum-coefficient models hold as ambient and supply conditions change in flight, simulates two actuator types at sea level and ten kilometres, and reports that the momentum coefficient collapses under a scaling parameter. **This article could not read which parameter.** No claim of novelty is made.

**Withdrawn in the primary-reference pass, and the correction reverses a correction.** The equation-density pass computed this aircraft's Reynolds number, compared it with the low-Reynolds experiments whose thresholds the article had borrowed, and concluded that those thresholds were the part most likely to be wrong at flight scale. **A report read in full in this pass tested the method at chord Reynolds numbers as high as forty million and states that the Reynolds number has a very weak effect on the pressure distributions and spectra of a fully turbulent baseline** \[[Seifert and Pack 2000][research_seifert_pack_2000]\]. **The X-65A flies below the demonstrated range, not beyond it**, so the strong form of the Reynolds argument is withdrawn and the narrow form, that transferring a threshold differs from transferring a phenomenon, is what remains.

**Withdrawn during drafting, and recorded rather than quietly fixed.** The first version of this analysis asserted that the classical separation-control regime requires a momentum coefficient between 0.005 and 0.02, computed that engine bleed cannot reach it, and made that the central difficulty. **The band was this article's invention.** The published review reports the separation-control to super-circulation threshold at three to five percent and a measured lift response at 0.08 percent, which the bleed budget reaches wing-referenced. **The conclusion reversed and the reversal is favourable to the aircraft.**

**Inference, marked as such.** That the Phase 0 to Phase 1 downselect occurred in mid-2021 is inferred from two option exercises in June and August 2021 and the absence of any option exercise on the third contract. No source consulted states a date. **That Phase 3 is worth about fifty-two million dollars** follows from subtracting the reported Phase 2 figure from the obligated total on one contract, and the press figure and the award record are not the same kind of quantity. **That the fiscal year 2026 request of 4.000 million dollars is a placeholder** is inferred from its being the only round number among seven figures carrying three decimals.

**Assumptions this article makes and could be wrong about.** That the air comes from engine bleed rather than a dedicated compressor, which is undetermined in the record and which decides whether the altitude result applies at all. That the engine operates at constant corrected flow across the altitude band. That the jet is choked, which the computed nozzle pressure ratio supports with an order of magnitude of margin under the bleed architecture. That the reference area is sixteen square metres, which is the middle of a sweep. That the effectors blow rather than suck, where the official definition admits both. That the amplification lies between ten and a hundred.

**What the record does not settle.** Where the pressurised air comes from. What the wing area, plenum pressure, bleed fraction or effector geometry are. What the aircraft weighs, to within 200 pound. Whether the planform is best described as joined tandem, diamond or triangular, three sources giving three answers. What the bleed costs in thrust. Whether the restructured co-investment is recorded anywhere public. **And whether any of this works, which is what the aircraft is for.**

**Written from current knowledge, dated 10 December 2025.** The bibliography carries publications later than that dateline, including a substantial 2026 literature on sweeping jets, fluidic oscillators and rudderless flight control. **No claim in the prose rests on anything after the dateline**, and the survey's own year range is the one place a later year appears in the body, where it describes the bibliography rather than the aircraft.

## Out of Scope

**The rotorcraft literature, deliberately.** Circulation control on a stopped rotor was flown, and the X-Wing programme is the obvious ancestor of a vehicle controlled by blowing rather than by surfaces. **The gate excludes rotorcraft because `hingeless` on the reports server is two hundred records of rotor hubs**, and the exclusion takes the X-Wing with it. That is a cost of a necessary guard and it is stated rather than hidden.

**Shock and boundary-layer interaction control.** The audit surfaced thirty-one such records and they were deliberately refused. The subject is supersonic inlets and hypersonic ramps, and the X-65A is limited to Mach 0.7. **Refusing them is a judgement and the judgement is recorded.**

**Thrust vectoring, except the fluidic kind.** Deflecting a propulsion jet is not controlling the flow over a lifting surface, so thirty-five thrust-vectoring records were refused while fluidic thrust vectoring was kept.

**The bleed penalty in thrust, quantitatively.** Deferred because it needs an engine deck.

**Suction-based flow control.** Deferred because its figure of merit is a flow coefficient and the identity this article rests on does not hold for it.

**Reynolds-number scaling of the amplification.** Deferred because it is the question the aircraft exists to answer and no analysis can substitute for it.

**The structural and aeroelastic consequences of a joined tandem wing.** Deferred, and the literature is cited.

**Noise.** Blowing is loud and the circulation-control literature carries a substantial acoustic component. Out of scope here.

## Conclusion

**The X-65A exists to find out whether a jet of air can do a hinge's job, and the arithmetic of the air says the answer depends almost entirely on Mach number.**

The momentum a bleed-fed effector can deliver is **exactly independent of altitude**, because corrected mass flow and compressible dynamic pressure both scale with ambient pressure, and because a choked jet fed from compressor air gains in velocity exactly what corrected flow loses in the square root of temperature. **Two cancellations meet and nothing about the atmosphere survives.** What survives is the inlet's own total-pressure recovery divided by the square of the Mach number, a function with an exact minimum at Mach $\sqrt{2}$ that this aircraft never reaches.

**So the authority ratio against a conventional surface falls as the aircraft accelerates and is flat as it climbs.** At an amplification of 30 against a surface worth 0.05 in lift coefficient, flow control wins below Mach 0.485 and loses above, delivering 5.15 times the hinge's authority at Mach 0.2 and 56.7 percent of it at Mach 0.7. **For a conventional aeroplane the hard case for control power is slow flight. For this one it is fast flight**, and a flight-test programme that expands its envelope upward happens to walk from the easy end to the hard one.

**The wing-referenced momentum coefficient sits inside the range that oscillatory separation control was demonstrated with at this aircraft's own Reynolds number**, 0.03 to 0.32 percent at sixteen million, and at that experiment's Mach number of 0.25 the same budget delivers 1.74 times its highest quoted value. **So the air is sufficient where the method is proved and marginal only where this aircraft wants to fly**, and the factor between those two conditions is the Mach dependence and nothing else. The super-circulation regime would still take more core flow than the engine has, which is why this is a control programme and not a high-lift one. **The concentration factor is what makes a moment affordable where a lift is not.**

**And the open problem was named in 2000.** The report that demonstrated the method at these Reynolds numbers proposed, among its future directions, overcoming the lack of sufficient control authority especially at high speeds \[[Seifert and Pack 2000][research_seifert_pack_2000]\]. **This article derived that constraint and did not discover it.**

**And the aircraft's most consequential design decision is not aerodynamic.** It carries two complete, equinumerous control systems and locks one out progressively. [The previous article][related_post_a361_invocon_x64] established that two vehicles cannot be differenced because the difference sought is smaller than the differences not sought. **The X-65A performs the subtraction inside one airframe**, which is why it can return a number rather than an impression. The 16,384 configurations that follow from fourteen surfaces are a red herring, because the quantity to identify is a model with 315 terms and that is about 11 flights.

**None of it has flown.** The programme has asked for 200.507 million dollars across seven budget years and expects 179.416 million, has been funded below its request in every year since fiscal year 2021 at a mean shortfall of 16.29 percent, promised its critical design review in four consecutive books for three different fiscal years, and was paused and restructured into an arrangement the federal award record cannot represent. **Its period of performance expired 69 days before this article's dateline with the fuselage unfinished.**

**The last thing to say is about the programme's name.** It is called CRANE, for Control of Revolutionary Aircraft with Novel Effectors, and its own acronym returns a logging-crane fatality and two books of nursery rhymes. The phrase `novel effectors` returns fungal pathogens. **The words that find this aircraft's actual prior art are `hingeless control effectors`, which no document in the programme uses**, and even those mean helicopter rotor hubs if you ask the wrong registry. **An aircraft meant to break a century-old paradigm was given a name under which the work it descends from cannot be found.**
